LLY — Evidence appendix
All harvested proof points · company first, then peers

Evidence

Claims by section
business-model
  • Tirzepatide supplied 98.3% of the three-year revenue increase, 99.5% of the FY2025 increase and 94.3% of the Q1 2026 increase
  • FY2025 franchise scripts of 70-84M at about $60 each implies franchise cost of sales of $4,200M-$5,040M against reported consolidated cost of sales of $11,052M; the residual implies a non-incretin gross margin of 76%-79%, consistent with a book of Trulicity, insulins, Verzenio, Taltz and a Jardiance collaboration share.
  • The consequence is that marginal gross margin on an incremental script is far above the ~86% average, which is why cutting the LillyDirect price from $499 to $299-449 still grows gross profit dollars, and why the binding constraint is demand-side rather than cost.
  • Cumulative FY2022-FY2025 incremental margin is 58.5%. Because the incretin franchise supplied 99.5% of FY2025 revenue growth, that realized 63% is, to a first approximation, the franchise's incremental margin after paying for the R&D and commercial infrastructure of the whole company.
  • covered-channel net revenue per script was roughly $630 in Q1 2025 and roughly $673 in Q1 2026 (range $632-$714 across a $350-$450 self-pay realization), while the blended figure declined from about $607 to $550 normalized.
  • US rebates, discounts and returns reduced net sales by $62,135M in 2025 against $43,481M of reported US revenue, implying US gross sales of $105,616M
  • three wholesalers - McKesson, Cencora and Cardinal Health - each accounted for between 16% and 24% of consolidated revenue and between 20% and 29% of receivables in 2025, and Lilly does not say which is which.
  • US Verzenio runs roughly 21,700–25,000 patient-years at $138,000–$160,000 of net price against $1,000–$4,000 of product cost, a 97–99% product gross margin
  • Jardiance ($3,431M) is not a product sale at all but a contractual share of Boehringer Ingelheim's net sales, running only through 31 December 2028 in the most significant markets, and reported growth is flattered: strip the $370M 2025 amendment benefit and the $200M milestone and underlying revenue declined 5.9%.
  • Kisunla and Emgality are the same modality made the same way at nearly the same cost per gram, and their economics diverge on channel rather than on clinical merit
capital-allocation
  • Lilly generated $52,085M of free cash flow and spent $93,271M on business development, dividends and buybacks combined, so roughly one dollar in three of that spending over twelve years came from borrowing rather than from operations.
capitalization
  • Total debt grew from $16,239M at the end of 2022 to $42,503M at the end of 2025 and $43,370M at 31 March 2026 — a 162% increase against a 227% increase in EBITDA, so gross leverage declined from 1.88x to 1.50x and to about 1.27x on a trailing-twelve-month basis through Q1 2026.
competitive-advantage
  • Lilly earns returns far above any plausible cost of capital and holds a real, multi-layer advantage in the incretin franchise, but the advantage is being converted into volume rather than price, and the price line is declining fast enough to be the central question about durability.
  • The cost advantage over Novo Nordisk is small: Lilly's Q1 2026 gross margin of 81.9% reported and 82.6% non-GAAP sits one to two points above Novo's adjusted 80.6%, not the ten-point gap a durable scale-cost advantage would produce, and Novo bought three Catalent manufacturing sites for $11.7bn in December 2024 to close the capacity gap.
  • free cash flow margin was 13.8% in FY2025 against a 45.6% core operating margin, because capital expenditure ran at 12.0% of revenue and inventory rose from $4.3bn to $13.7bn in three years.
  • It is the middle case, an advantage that earns excess returns today and is defensible for now but open to erosion, and it sits at the strong end of that case, because the erosion vector is price and policy rather than share loss.
  • The closest reference class is **Gilead Sciences** in hepatitis C and HIV: a best-in-class, patent-protected molecule in a category the company effectively created, throwing off enormous cash on a finite clock, whose realized price declined hard once a credible rival and concentrated payers arrived.
  • core ROIC sat in a 33-40% band across 2018-2023 and then stepped up to 47.9% in FY2025 (35.1% all-in on ending invested capital, 42.5% on average invested capital)
  • Against the US peer median the gross-margin gap went from +2 points (2020) to +8.7 points (FY2025) and about +9 points in Q1 2026; the operating-margin gap went from (5) points (2020) to +11 points (FY2025) and about +16 points in Q1 2026; the ROIC gap from +14 points to +18 points.
  • Against Novo the picture is much less comfortable: the gross-margin gap is two points and stable, the FY2025 ROIC gap is half a point (35.1% all-in against 34.6%), and Lilly's advantage comes almost entirely from being on the right side of the growth divide rather than from a structural cost or price edge.
competitive-landscape
  • Two firms sell every branded incretin analog of consequence. Concentration on the two-company revenue pool is as high as a two-firm market allows, with an implied Herfindahl index above 5,000 on every basis.
  • One 2026 source puts 2025 global GLP-1 sales at approximately $132bn and tirzepatide products at over $62bn [web]; the two producers of essentially every branded incretin analog in the world reported $76.2bn of combined FY2025 revenue. Any penetration or TAM work built off the larger figure overstates the base by roughly 1.7x.
  • Zepbound at ~7.0M prescriptions and $591 realized, Mounjaro at ~9.0M and about $470, Trulicity at ~1.2M — 17.2M Lilly prescriptions at a disclosed 60.1% share gives a total US incretin analogs market of about 28.6M prescriptions in Q1 2026, or 2.2M a week.
  • That combination explains the flattening consolidated share line: Lilly is still taking share in the market that has stopped expanding, and holding share in the market that is doubling.
  • Generic semaglutide is already marketed in India from March 2026 and Canada from January 2026, in markets holding roughly a third of the world's adults living with obesity [web].
  • Within one quarter Novo's pill was running at roughly 28% of Zepbound's weekly US prescription volume.
critical-points
  • Non-incretin revenue was $28,058M in 2022 and $28,672M in 2025, compounding at 0.7% a year. Two of the five franchises are in absolute decline.
  • The ten-year gross-margin climb from 73.1% in 2016 has ended; whether the operating leverage continues is a spending decision rather than a manufacturing constraint.
  • FY2025 free cash flow of $8,972M is 43% of net income; after the $3,008M of cash spent on in-process-R&D asset acquisitions - which sits in investing while the charge is added back in operating - it is $5,964M, or 29%.
expectations
  • The headline disagreement is that consensus expands operating margin about nine points between 2026 and 2031 while management has published no multi-year target of any kind since 13 December 2022, and the base rates say no pharmaceutical company has ever expanded operating margin from above 40%.
  • The December 2021 Investment Community Meeting set the next one: top-tier volume-driven growth through at least 2030, mid-single-digit annual net price declines, gross margin around 80%, and operating margin expanding to the mid-to-upper 30s with SG&A as the primary lever. Three of the four were beaten. The price commitment broke by roughly 3x.
  • Consensus therefore assigns Lilly 92.6% of the incremental revenue of the three named incretin players over four years.
financial-history
  • Operating leverage came predominantly from selling costs rather than from gross margin. Marketing, selling and administrative grew 70% in dollars while revenue grew 207%; R&D grew 151% in dollars, so R&D intensity declined only 4.6 points.
forward-model
  • Revenue compounds 11.1% a year from FY2025 to FY2031 and 6.6% to FY2035.
  • Base-case incretin revenue grows 168% between 2025 and 2031 while net revenue per US script declines 30% and per ex-US script declines 47%, so total scripts have to grow 4.7x for revenue to grow 2.7x.
  • Moving R&D and marketing to their bear settings removes $10.1bn of FY2035 operating income without touching a single unit of demand. At a 78% gross margin the cost structure is a discretionary spending decision rather than a manufacturing constraint.
  • Against the base rates, the base case lands at the top of the pharmaceutical revenue distribution rather than beyond it, and holds a margin that is already the sector record rather than expanding it further.
history-inflections
  • Cost discipline at Lilly arrives after a research failure rather than after a revenue decline: three years of falling revenue produced no restructuring; one failed Phase 3 produced a $1.67bn charge within twelve months.
  • Lilly priced into the volume rather than defending price, and the pre-built capacity from the prior inflection is what makes the concession survivable.
m-and-a
  • Lilly's deal *structure* is disciplined and its deal *strike rate* is poor.
  • Six transactions between 2018 and 2024 bought clinical assets at Phase 2 or Phase 3 for a combined $8.8bn, and none has produced an approved product
management-claims
  • The 95% ex-US volume growth against a 25% price decline comes from newly-launched countries where penetration started near zero, which is a first-fill effect rather than a re-order elasticity.
  • Lilly's consolidated realized price decline is roughly 2x Amgen's in FY2025 and roughly 4x it at the Q1 2026 run rate.
  • Verzenio's dollar share of the class went from 41.8% to 39.1% in one year while the class grew 15.2%. Kisqali sold $1,516M in Q1 2026 against Verzenio's $1,302M, the first quarter in which Verzenio was not the largest CDK4/6 inhibitor.
  • Zepbound's 13-week rolling US share of the branded obesity market has been approximately 64%, 62%, 64%, 64% across the last four quarters. Lilly's actual share gains came from type 2 diabetes, where Mounjaro went from 42% to about 51% over the same period.
management-incentives
  • The seven-product basket is 67.0% of revenue and excludes the declining, price-controlled third of the business — Trulicity, Jardiance, the insulins. Erosion there produces no bonus outcome in either direction.
risks
  • The mix is shifting toward the fastest-declining price: outside-US was 33% of FY2025 revenue and 39% in Q1 2026, growing 81% against the US at 43%
secular-trends
  • Volume growth and price decline are now the same variable, because every incremental access channel Lilly is opening prices below the current blended realized price
  • Adjusting FY2023 to $32,095M: 2023 organic growth was +12.5%, not +19.6%, and 2024's was +40.3%, not +32.0%. Anyone chaining the reported series understates the 2024 acceleration by eight points.
  • Neither basis supports a claim that Lilly's non-incretin portfolio is outgrowing the industry. The 45% headline is the molecule.
  • Novo holds a volume majority and a value minority. The reconciling variable is price per unit: Novo's volume sits disproportionately in lower-priced international and Saxenda channels.
  • Whichever year it lands, the economically binding date is roughly four to five years before the 2036 patent, and Lilly is opening Medicare access on 1 July 2026, which builds precisely the Part D gross-spend base that determines selection ranking.
technology
  • Three mutually contradictory pharmacologies reach roughly the same weight loss, which is direct evidence that dual agonism is a route to the destination rather than the only route.
  • Foundayo delivered 12.4% weight loss at the top dose in ATTAIN-1 over 72 weeks. Novo's oral semaglutide 25mg delivered 16.6% in OASIS-4 over 64 weeks and was approved in the US in December 2025, four months earlier [web].
  • roughly a 16-step convergent small-molecule synthesis instead of solid-phase peptide synthesis, estimated 30-50% below peptide-competitor manufacturing cost, no cold chain, and no sterile fill-finish requirement
  • Aseptic fill-finish capacity is regulator-qualified line by line and product by product and cannot be bought forward.
  • R&D intensity has declined for three years - 27.3% (FY2023), 24.4% (FY2024), 20.5% (FY2025), 17.7% (Q1 2026) - because revenue outran a rising R&D dollar.
value-chain
  • The intermediaries here earn a fee on volume at thin margins, and their power is to deny access rather than to capture margin. That is why Lilly's response was a parallel channel rather than a price fight.
  • The self-pay channel is volume-accretive and access-protective, not price-accretive, costing roughly $273 per script against the reimbursed alternative on this arithmetic.
  • The decline is a population change, not a reduction in obligation: take-or-pay on commercial-stage incretin manufacturing dropped out of the disclosed figure and has not been disclosed since.
  • the oral GLP-1 management describes as the next leg of growth is the one product with a permanent third-party claim on its gross margin, while the injectable that produced 56% of FY2025 revenue has none

Tirzepatide supplied 98.3% of the three-year revenue increase, 99.5% of the FY2025 increase and 94.3% of the Q1 2026 increase

For the Jardiance product family, we and Boehringer Ingelheim generally share equally in certain significant ongoing development and commercialization costs, and we record our portion of the development and commercialization costs as research and development expense and marketing, selling, and administrative expense, respectively. We receive a royalty on net sales of the Jardiance product family in the most significant markets and recognize the royalty as collaboration and other revenue. Boehringer Ingelheim is entitled to potential performance payments depending on the net sales of the Jardiance product family; therefore, our reported revenue for Jardiance may be reduced by any potential performance payments we make related to this product family. The royalty received by us related to the Jardiance product family may also be increased or decreased depending on whether net sales for this product family exceed or fall below certain thresholds.

Gross margin was 74.8% in 2025 compared with 76.3% in 2024. — Merck & Co., Inc., Annual Report on Form 10-K for FY2025, 2026-02-24

Mounjaro/ Zepbound compound patent U.S. 2036 major European countries 2037 Japan 2040 data protection U.S. 2027 major European countries 2033 Japan
— Eli Lilly and Company, Annual Report on Form 10-K for the fiscal year ended December 31, 2025, 2026-02-12
filingsupports
As a result, when a branded non-biologic pharmaceutical loses its market exclusivity, it normally faces intense price competition from generic forms of the product, which can result in the loss of a significant portion of the branded product's revenue in a very short period of time. Loss of market exclusivity for competitive products may also shift market conditions for other branded products in the same therapeutic class. Moreover, governments in some countries leverage generic entrants to drive price concessions through the utilization of volume-based procurement bidding and other measures.
— Eli Lilly and Company, Annual Report on Form 10-K for the fiscal year ended December 31, 2025, 2026-02-12
filingsupports
Intellectual property threats across international markets include policies which favor domestic competitors or are otherwise disadvantageous to our products, challenges to our patents' validity in foreign courts, and government policies that force technology transfer or undermine or nullify patent protections under the guise of health emergencies or other public interests. Furthermore, intellectual property protection in certain jurisdictions is weak and we face heightened risks to our intellectual property rights in these jurisdictions, including competition with generic or counterfeit versions of our products at or relatively shortly after launch as well as the risk of diversion of generic or counterfeit products from these jurisdictions into others.
— Eli Lilly and Company, Annual Report on Form 10-K for the fiscal year ended December 31, 2025, 2026-02-12

MID- TO LONG-TERM OUTLOOK — ROBUST GROWTH AND MARGIN EXPANSION OPPORTUNITY THROUGHOUT THE DECADE. Revenue: Top-tier, volume-driven revenue growth through the balance of the decade. Continued growth of marketed products. Multiple new launches. Limited patent exposure in the next five years. Mid-single digit annual net price declines, on average. Gross Margin %: Maintain ~80% gross margin as manufacturing productivity actions offset pricing headwinds, increasing product mix of biologics and expanded footprint to support robust growth. Operating Margin: Continued opportunity for expansion. SG&A: Lever for margin expansion over time. R&D: Prioritizing long-term growth and continued investment in innovation. Capital Allocation Priorities: Fund pipeline and execute new launches. Leverage external innovation. Increase dividend, in line with earnings growth over time. Return excess capital to shareholders via share repurchase.
— 2021 Investment Community Meeting, 2021-12-15

— Pfizer Inc. Form 10-K, fiscal year ended December 31, 2025, 2026-02-26

Base-case incretin revenue grows 168% between 2025 and 2031 while net revenue per US script declines 30% and per ex-US script declines 47%, so total scripts have to grow 4.7x for revenue to grow 2.7x.

On October 6, 2023, Amgen completed its acquisition of Horizon by acquiring all of the outstanding shares of Horizon for $116.50 per share in cash, representing a total consideration of approximately $27.8 billion. Horizon is a global biotechnology company focused on the discovery, development and commercialization of medicines that address critical needs of patients impacted by rare, autoimmune and severe inflammatory diseases. The acquisition, which was accounted for as a business combination, aligns with Amgen's core strategy of delivering innovative medicines that make a significant difference for patients suffering from serious diseases and strengthens Amgen's leading rare disease portfolio by adding first-in-class, early-in-lifecycle medicines, including TEPEZZA for TED, KRYSTEXXA for chronic refractory gout and UPLIZNA for neuromyelitis optica spectrum disorder.
— Amgen (10-K narrative), Amgen Inc. FY2025 Form 10-K, Note 4: Acquisition — Acquisition of Horizon Therapeutics plc, 2026-02-13

filingsupports
The Inflation Reduction Act of 2022 (IRA) requires: (i) the government to set prices for select high expenditure Medicare Part D drugs (prices effective beginning in 2026) and Part B drugs (prices effective beginning in 2028) that are more than nine years (for small-molecule drugs) or 13 years (for biological products) from their FDA approval, (ii) manufacturers to pay a rebate for Medicare Part B and Part D drugs when prices for those drugs increase faster than inflation beginning in 2022 for Part D and 2023 for Part B
— AbbVie Inc. Form 10-K, fiscal year 2025, 2026-02

Peer: Mewburn Ellis

websupports ✦ best
a small molecule like orforglipron is a roughly 16-step convergent organic synthesis from common starting materials. ... a peptide GLP-1 needs solid-phase synthesis (semaglutide is a 31-mer with non-natural modifications and a lipid chain) or recombinant expression followed by complex modification. ... The supply chain is shorter, the cost-of-goods is lower, and the global capacity is essentially uncapped. Contrast this with the peptides which faced major supply issues between 2023-2025.
— Andrew Pitts, Orforglipron FDA Approval: Why the First Small-Molecule GLP-1 Pill Matters (Mewburn Ellis Forward), 2026-06-02

Large-scale solid-phase peptide synthesis (SPPS) is capital-intensive, solvent-hungry, and difficult to push toward commodity scale. Industry benchmarking places typical peptide processes at roughly 13,000 kilograms of input per kilogram of API. ... Small molecules average 168 to 308 kg of input per kilogram of API, and even above many biologics that hover around 8,300 kilograms of input per kilogram of API.
— Achim Link, Oral GLP-1s Won't Win on Convenience - They'll Win on CMC (The Medicine Maker), 2026-01-28
webqualifies
Because the bioavailability of oral semaglutide is on the order of one percent, oral use demands far more API per patient than injections...oral regimen being studied for obesity is a daily dose of 50 milligrams, while injectable semaglutide is given weekly as low as 2.0 milligrams. ... If a daily small molecule can deliver comparable results, we can produce far more doses per reactor-hour, at lower cost, in more locations.
— Achim Link, Oral GLP-1s Won't Win on Convenience - They'll Win on CMC (The Medicine Maker), 2026-01-28

Aseptic fill-finish capacity is regulator-qualified line by line and product by product and cannot be bought forward.

filingsupports
Direct-to-consumer ("DTC") sales of prescription drugs by pharmaceutical companies is a growing trend in the United States. By implementing DTC sales platforms, pharmaceutical companies can advertise, or sell, their own branded drugs directly to patients, bypassing traditional distribution channels and intermediaries, including pharmacies and PBMs. DTC platforms may also increase demand for expensive, brand-name drugs that may not provide significant clinical benefit over more cost-effective alternatives. As a result, the DTC trend may increase overall health care costs for consumers and adversely impact the performance of the Company's Pharmacy & Consumer Wellness and Health Services segments.
— CVS Health Corporation FY2025 Form 10-K, 2025-12-31
